  (function() {
    var header = document.getElementById("__rptl-header"),
        burgerState = document.getElementById("__rptl-header-burger-state"),
        cookiebanner = document.getElementById("__rptl-cookiebanner"),
        accept = document.getElementById("__rptl-cookiebanner-accept"),
        reject = document.getElementById("__rptl-cookiebanner-reject");

    if (document.cookie.indexOf("cookiebanner_accepted=") === -1) {
      cookiebanner.classList.remove("__rptl-cookiebanner-hidden");
    }

    accept.addEventListener("click", function (e) {
      document.cookie = "cookiebanner_accepted=1; path=/; max-age=" + 60 * 60 * 24 * 365 * 2;

      if (typeof window.gtag !== "undefined") {
        window.gtag("consent", "update", {
          analytics_storage: "granted",
        });
      }

      cookiebanner.classList.add("__rptl-cookiebanner-hidden");
    });

    reject.addEventListener("click", function (e) {
      document.cookie = "cookiebanner_accepted=0; path=/; max-age=" + 60 * 60 * 24 * 365 * 2;

      cookiebanner.classList.add("__rptl-cookiebanner-hidden");
    });

    document.addEventListener("click", function (e) {
      if (!header.contains(e.target)) {
        burgerState.checked = false;
      }
    });
  })();

      <div id="docs-container">
        <div id="main-window">
          <section id="content" role="main"><h1>Raspberry Pi OS
</h1>
            

<div id="preamble">
<div class="sectionbody">
<div id="introduction" class="paragraph">
<p>Raspberry Pi OS is the official operating system (OS) for Raspberry Pi computers and is free. We recommend Raspberry Pi OS for most Raspberry Pi use cases.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Raspberry Pi OS is based on the <strong>Debian</strong> Linux distribution and supports over 69,000 Debian packages. Raspberry Pi follows a staggered version of the <a href="https://wiki.debian.org/DebianReleases">Debian release cycle</a>. Releases happen roughly once every two years. The latest version of Raspberry Pi OS is based on <a href="https://www.raspberrypi.com/news/trixie-the-new-version-of-raspberry-pi-os/">Debian Trixie</a>. The previous version was based on <a href="https://www.raspberrypi.com/news/bookworm-the-new-version-of-raspberry-pi-os/">Debian Bookworm</a>.</p>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect1">
<h2 id="os-installation">
<a class="anchor" href="#os-installation"></a>OS installation</h2>
<div class="sectionbody"></div>
<div class="paragraph edit-link">
<p>Edit this <a href="https://github.com/raspberrypi/documentation/blob/master/documentation/asciidoc/computers/os/rpi-os-introduction.adoc">on GitHub</a></p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Raspberry Pi OS is installed using Raspberry Pi Imager, available for Windows, Mac, and Linux. For instructions, see <a href="../computers/getting-started.html#imager-install">Install using Imager</a>. You can also download a disk image (ready-to-use file) of Raspberry Pi OS directly from <a href="https://raspberrypi.com/software/operating-systems">our website</a> and then write it to an SD card to start using your Raspberry Pi.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>If you’re running a previous version of <strong>Raspberry Pi OS</strong> and want to use the latest major version, don’t perform an in-place update (updating the current system without reinstalling). Instead, start again by installing a new image of Raspberry Pi OS. For instructions, see <a href="../computers/getting-started.html#imager-install">Install using Imager</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="admonitionblock warning">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Warning</div>
</td>
<td class="content">
Back up any important files from your old OS that you don’t want to lose. Installing a new OS overwrites everything on the SD card.
</td>
</tr></table></div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="editions">
<a class="anchor" href="#editions"></a>Editions</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Raspberry Pi OS comes in three editions:</p>
</div>
<div class="ulist">
<ul><li>
<p><strong>Raspberry Pi OS</strong> (desktop with basic software) is the recommended edition offered in Raspberry Pi Imager. It provides a graphical desktop environment and includes applications such as Chromium and Firefox web browsers, VLC media player, and the Thonny Python environment.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Raspberry Pi OS Full</strong> (desktop with basic software and recommended applications), provides the same graphical desktop environment and applications as the default <strong>Raspberry Pi OS</strong> edition, but also includes the LibreOffice suite, KiCad, a Scratch programming environment, and many other applications.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Raspberry Pi OS Lite</strong> (a command-line-only version without a graphical desktop). This edition is useful for headless servers, embedded systems, or older Raspberry Pi models, such as the original Raspberry Pi, Raspberry Pi 2, and Raspberry Pi Zero.</p>
</li>
</ul></div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="architecture">
<a class="anchor" href="#architecture"></a>Architecture</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Raspberry Pi OS is available in both 64-bit and 32-bit versions. Choose the version that best matches your Raspberry Pi device’s hardware:</p>
</div>
<div class="ulist">
<ul><li>
<p>The <strong>64-bit</strong> version is designed for newer Raspberry Pi models that have 64-bit processors, like Raspberry Pi 3, 4, and 5. It can run both 64-bit and 32-bit software, but can also handle more memory (RAM) and is faster for tasks that require more computing power.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>The <strong>32-bit</strong> version is designed for older Raspberry Pi models that have 32-bit processors, like the original Raspberry Pi, Raspberry Pi 2, and Raspberry Pi Zero. It uses less memory and might be more compatible with older software and hardware.</p>
</li>
</ul></div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect1">
<h2 id="manage-software-and-firmware-updates">
<a class="anchor" href="#manage-software-and-firmware-updates"></a>Manage software and firmware updates</h2>
<div class="sectionbody"></div>
<div class="paragraph edit-link">
<p>Edit this <a href="https://github.com/raspberrypi/documentation/blob/master/documentation/asciidoc/computers/os/updating.adoc">on GitHub</a></p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>There are two ways to update your current operating system:</p>
</div>
<div class="ulist">
<ul><li>
<p><strong>APT-based</strong>. We recommend this for most users. Use it to update the software, kernel, and stable firmware of your current Raspberry Pi OS installation. For instructions, see <a href="#update-software">Update your current Raspberry Pi software</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong><code>rpi-update</code></strong>. Use this for experimental or pre-release kernel and firmware testing, development, or specific bug fixes. For instructions, see <a href="#rpi-update">Update pre-release firmware</a>.</p>
</li>
</ul></div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Normal firmware updates are included in APT updates (<code>raspi-firmware</code> package); <code>rpi-update</code> is only appropriate for developers and testers, or when you’re specifically instructed by Raspberry Pi engineers to use it.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>If, instead of updating your current operating system, you want to upgrade from a previous version of Raspberry Pi OS to a new version (for example, from Bookworm to Trixie), reinstall the operating system from a new image. For instructions, see <a href="#upgrade">Upgrade to a new major version</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="update-software">
<a class="anchor" href="#update-software"></a>Update your current Raspberry Pi software</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Keep the software on your Raspberry Pi up to date. This helps to keep your Raspberry Pi secure from <a href="https://cve.mitre.org/index.html">vulnerabilities</a> and ensures that you get the latest bug fixes. It also updates your Linux kernel and firmware.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The following instructions describe how to keep your <em>current</em> installation of <strong>Raspberry Pi OS</strong> up to date within the same major version of the operating system you’re running (for example, Trixie). They don’t upgrade your Raspberry Pi OS to a new major release (for example, from Bookworm to Trixie). For instructions on upgrading your OS, see <a href="#upgrade">Upgrade to a new major version</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>We recommend using APT to keep your Raspberry Pi software up to date. You can also use APT to find a specific package or tool, inspect a package’s metadata, install a specific package, and uninstall a specific package. For instructions, see <a href="#manage-software">Manage software packages</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To use APT to update your current Raspberry Pi software with the latest fixes, Linux kernel, and firmware, run the following commands regularly:</p>
</div>
<div class="olist arabic">
<ol class="arabic"><li>
<p><strong>Update your package list.</strong> APT maintains a list of available software sources in <code>/etc/apt/sources.list</code> and <code>/etc/apt/sources.list.d/</code>; run the following command to refresh your list of available software packages:</p>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>apt update</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Update your installed packages to their latest versions.</strong> Now that you have a list of what new versions are available, run the following command to download and install the updates. Because Raspberry Pi OS changes package dependencies more often that Debian, we recommend that you use <code>full-upgrade</code> instead of the standard <code>upgrade</code>:</p>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>apt full-upgrade</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Before continuing with the update, check that you have enough free disk space. If <code>sudo apt full-upgrade</code> says that it needs extra space to complete the update, you can check that your system has at least that much free space available using <code>df -h</code>. You can then clean up downloaded files to make more space as described in <a href="#clean">Clean up files</a>.</p>
</div>
</li>
</ol></div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="rpi-update">
<a class="anchor" href="#rpi-update"></a>Update pre-release firmware</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p><code>rpi-update</code> is a command-line tool used to install experimental or pre-release firmware on a Raspberry Pi. On the Rasberry Pi 4 and 5, it also updates the EEPROM bootloader.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p><code>rpi-update</code> downloads the latest pre-release version of the Linux kernel, its matching modules, device tree files, and the latest version of the VideoCore firmware. It then installs these files into an existing Raspberry Pi OS install.</p>
</div>
<div class="admonitionblock warning">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Warning</div>
</td>
<td class="content">
Pre-release firmware isn’t guaranteed to work and can introduce instability, rendering your system unreliable or unbootable.
</td>
</tr></table></div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>All source data used by <code>rpi-update</code> comes from the <a href="https://github.com/raspberrypi/rpi-firmware">rpi-firmware repository</a>, which contains a subset of data from the official <a href="https://github.com/raspberrypi/firmware">firmware repository</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p><code>rpi-update</code> is intended for use by developers and engineers for testing, development, and specific bug fixes. In most cases, you don’t need to use <code>rpi-update</code>, and you should only do so if recommended by a Raspberry Pi engineer; for routine updates, we strongly recommend using APT instead, as described in <a href="#update-software">Update your current Raspberry Pi software</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="olist arabic">
<ol class="arabic"><li>
<p>Make a backup of your system.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Run the following command to initiate the update:</p>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>rpi-update</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</li>
<li>
<p>Reboot your Raspberry Pi for the changes to take effect:</p>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>reboot</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</li>
</ol></div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>For more information, see <a href="https://pip.raspberrypi.com/documents/RP-003476-WP">Updating Raspberry Pi firmware</a>.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="downgrade">
<a class="anchor" href="#downgrade"></a>Downgrade firmware to the last stable release</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>If updating to the latest pre-release firmware causes issues, you can revert to the last stable firmware and then reboot for the downgrade to take effect:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>apt update
<span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>apt <span class="nb">install</span> <span class="nt">--reinstall</span> raspi-firmware
<span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>reboot</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>This reinstalls the officially supported firmware from Raspberry Pi OS, restoring system stability.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="manage-software">
<a class="anchor" href="#manage-software"></a>Manage software packages</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>From the Terminal, <strong>APT</strong> is the default package manager used to install, update, and remove software within the Raspberry Pi OS you’re currently running. You control it using Terminal commands like <code>apt-cache</code> and <code>apt install</code>.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>From the Desktop, you can use the <strong>Preferences &gt; Add / Remove Software</strong> tool to manage software using the graphical interface.</p>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="search-for-software">
<a class="anchor" href="#search-for-software"></a>Search for software</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To find a specific package or tool, use <code>apt-cache search</code> followed by a keyword. This returns a list of packages with names and descriptions that include the keyword. For example, to show all packages related to <code>raspi</code>:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span>apt-cache search raspi</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="view-package-details">
<a class="anchor" href="#view-package-details"></a>View package details</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To view detailed information about a package, use <code>apt-cache show</code> followed by a package name. This displays information such as package version, size, and dependencies. For example, to view more detailed information about the <code>raspi-config</code> package:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span>apt-cache show raspi-config</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="install-a-package">
<a class="anchor" href="#install-a-package"></a>Install a package</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To install a specific package on your Raspberry Pi, use <code>sudo apt install</code> followed by the package name:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>apt <span class="nb">install</span> &lt;package-name&gt;</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>This returns the amount of disk space that the package needs and you’re asked to confirm the installation. To confirm the installation, enter <code>Y</code> followed by <strong>Enter</strong> or <strong>Return</strong> on your keyboard. To skip the confirmation prompt and automatically answer 'yes' at the time that you install the package, add <code>-y</code> to the command:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>apt <span class="nb">install</span> <span class="nt">-y</span> &lt;package-name&gt;</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="uninstall-a-package">
<a class="anchor" href="#uninstall-a-package"></a>Uninstall a package</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To uninstall a specific package on your Raspberry Pi, use <code>sudo apt remove</code> followed by the package name:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>apt remove &lt;package-name&gt;</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>This returns the amount of disk space that removing the package gives you and you’re asked to confirm the removal of the package. To confirm the removal, enter <code>Y</code> followed by <strong>Enter</strong> or <strong>Return</strong> on your keyboard to confirm the installation. To skip the confirmation prompt and automatically answer 'yes' at the time that you uninstall the package, add <code>-y</code> to the command:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>apt remove <span class="nt">-y</span> &lt;package-name&gt;</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To completely remove all traces of the package, including configuration files, use <code>purge</code> instead of <code>remove</code>:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>apt purge &lt;package-name&gt;</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="check-disk-space">
<a class="anchor" href="#check-disk-space"></a>Check disk space</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Before installing or updating software on your Raspberry Pi, check the available storage using the following command:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>

</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="clean">
<a class="anchor" href="#clean"></a>Clean up files</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>APT stores downloaded packages (<code>.deb</code> files) in <code>/var/cache/apt/archives</code>. During installation, APT downloads these packages and then copies files from these packages to the correct installation locations. Depending on the software you’ve installed, package files can take up a lot of disk space. Use the following command to delete old downloaded package files:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">sudo </span>apt clean</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="upgrade">
<a class="anchor" href="#upgrade"></a>Upgrade to a new major version</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Rather than an in-place upgrade on your existing boot media, we strongly recommend that you use a clean install to upgrade the OS version on your Raspberry Pi, like moving from Bookworm to Trixie. This involves using <strong>Raspberry Pi Imager</strong> to flash a new OS image onto new boot media.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The following instructions require:</p>
</div>
<div class="ulist">
<ul><li>
<p>A new storage device for your boot media (typically a microSD card), and a way to connect it to a computer (for example, a USB card reader).</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>A computer to write a new OS image to your new boot media, and to store and move data between boot media.</p>
</li>
</ul></div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>The following instructions assume that you’re using a microSD card as your new boot media and Raspberry Pi Imager to install a new OS image onto that boot media. If you’re using a different type of storage for your boot media, modify the steps below as appropriate.</p>
</div>
<div class="olist arabic">
<ol class="arabic"><li>
<p><strong>Create a backup of your current boot media.</strong></p>
</li>
</ol></div>
<div class="admonitionblock warning">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Warning</div>
</td>
<td class="content">
This step permanently deletes all files on the storage device you choose to use. Back them up before continuing.
</td>
</tr></table></div>
<div class="olist arabic">
<ol class="arabic"><li>
<p><strong>Prepare a new storage device with the new OS.</strong> On a new storage device, install a new OS image with the latest Raspberry Pi OS version. For instructions, see <a href="../computers/getting-started.html#imager-install">Install using Imager</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Copy your existing files and configurations.</strong> Use a storage device to copy important files from your Raspberry Pi.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Insert the new boot media into your Raspberry Pi.</strong> Insert the storage device containing the new OS into your Raspberry Pi.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><strong>Boot your Raspberry Pi.</strong> Connect your Raspberry Pi to power and power it on.</p>
</li>
</ol></div>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="convert-raspberry-pi-os-lite-into-raspberry-pi-os-desktop">
<a class="anchor" href="#convert-raspberry-pi-os-lite-into-raspberry-pi-os-desktop"></a>Convert Raspberry Pi OS Lite into Raspberry Pi OS Desktop</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>You can use APT to add the packages that turn the Lite version into the Desktop version.</p>
</div>
<div class="olist arabic">
<ol class="arabic"><li>
<p>Install your preferred desktop windowing system.</p>
<div class="ulist">
<ul><li>

</div></button>
<div class="content">

</div>
</div>
</li>
</ol></div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>You now have all the packages that comprise Raspberry Pi OS Desktop.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>If you want to return to the Lite version of Raspberry Pi OS, you can remove these packages. Alternatively, to have the Lite experience without removing the desktop packages you’ve installed, follow the instructions in <a href="../computers/configuration.html#console-or-desktop">Boot to console or desktop</a> to set your Raspberry Pi to boot to the command line.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>You can also install the recommended applications included in Raspberry Pi OS Full by going to your Raspberry Pi main menu and selecting <strong>Preferences &gt; Recommended Software</strong>.</p>
</div>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect1">
<h2 id="play-audio-and-video">
<a class="anchor" href="#play-audio-and-video"></a>Play audio and video</h2>
<div class="sectionbody"></div>
<div class="paragraph edit-link">
<p>Edit this <a href="https://github.com/raspberrypi/documentation/blob/master/documentation/asciidoc/computers/os/playing-audio-and-video.adoc">on GitHub</a></p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Raspberry Pi OS comes with <a href="https://www.videolan.org/">VLC media player</a> pre-installed. You can use VLC to play video and audio files. VLC uses hardware acceleration in Raspberry Pi OS, and supports many popular audio and video file formats.</p>
</div>
<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="vlc-media-player">
<a class="anchor" href="#vlc-media-player"></a>VLC media player</h3>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="vlc-gui">
<a class="anchor" href="#vlc-gui"></a>VLC GUI</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To play an audio or video file from Raspberry Pi Desktop, double-click on a file in the file manager. This automatically launches VLC to play the file. Alternatively, from the <strong>Sound &amp; Video</strong> menu, launch <strong>VLC Media Player</strong>. Then, from the <strong>Media</strong> menu, select <strong>Open File…​</strong> and navigate to the file you want to play.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>By default, Raspberry Pi OS sends audio to your monitor over HDMI. To output audio to a different interface, such as the headphone jack or USB speakers, right-click on the speaker icon in the system tray and select an option.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="sect3">
<h4 id="vlc-cli">
<a class="anchor" href="#vlc-cli"></a><code>vlc</code> CLI</h4>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>You can also launch VLC from the command line. For the examples below, we used a short clip from Big Buck Bunny. To download this clip from Raspberry Pi, run the following command:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span>wget <span class="nt">--trust-server-names</span> http://rpltd.co/big-buck-bunny</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To play the clip in VLC from the command line, run the following command:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>

<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="specify-an-audio-output-device">
<a class="anchor" href="#specify-an-audio-output-device"></a>Specify an audio output device</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To force audio output to a particular device, pass the <code>alsa</code> value to the the <code>-A</code> option to use <a href="https://www.alsa-project.org/wiki/Main_Page">ALSA</a> audio output, and the <code>--alsa-audio-device</code> option to specify an audio output device:</p>
</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span>cvlc <span class="nt">--play-and-exit</span> <span class="nt">-A</span> alsa <span class="nt">--alsa-audio-device</span> &lt;alsa-device&gt; computer-startup-music.mp3</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Replace the <code>&lt;alsa-device&gt;</code> placeholder with one of the following options:</p>
</div>
<table class="tableblock frame-all grid-all stretch"><colgroup><col style="width: 50%;"><col style="width: 50%;"></colgroup><thead><tr><th class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">ALSA device</th>
<th class="tableblock halign-left valign-top">Description</th>
</tr></thead><tbody><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>sysdefault:CARD=Headphones</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">The headphone jack on Raspberry Pi 4 and earlier models</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>sysdefault:CARD=vc4hdmi</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">The HDMI output on devices with a single HDMI port (Zero models, CM4S, Compute Modules prior to CM4, and Flagship models prior to Raspberry Pi 4)</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>sysdefault:CARD=vc4hdmi0</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">The HDMI0 output on Flagship models since Raspberry Pi 4B, Compute Modules since CM4, and Keyboard models</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>sysdefault:CARD=vc4hdmi1</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">The HDMI1 output on Flagship models since Raspberry Pi 4B, Compute Modules since CM4, and Keyboard models</p></td>
</tr></tbody></table><div class="admonitionblock tip">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Tip</div>
</td>
<td class="content">

</div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>

<div class="sect2">
<h3 id="specify-a-video-output-device">
<a class="anchor" href="#specify-a-video-output-device"></a>Specify a video output device</h3>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>You can specify a video output using either the VLC GUI, or by using <code>--drm-vout-display</code> from the console.</p>
</div>
<div class="paragraph">
<p>To specify the video output from the console:</p>
</div>
<div class="olist arabic">
<ol class="arabic"><li>
<p>Boot your Raspberry Pi device to the <a href="../computers/configuration.html#console-or-desktop">console</a>.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p>Add the <code>--drm-vout-display</code> option:</p>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" id="Layer_1" data-name="Layer 1" viewbox="0 0 12 12"><rect x="1" y="6" width="5" height="1"></rect><path d="M10,1H8A1,1,0,0,0,7,0H6A1,1,0,0,0,5,1H3A1,1,0,0,0,2,2V5H3V2H4V3H9V2h1v9H3V8H2v3a1,1,0,0,0,1,1h7a1,1,0,0,0,1-1V2A1,1,0,0,0,10,1ZM6.5,2.25a.75.75,0,1,1,.75-.75A.76.76,0,0,1,6.5,2.25Z"></path><path d="M6.32,6.32,4.43,4.43A.25.25,0,0,0,4,4.6V8.4a.25.25,0,0,0,.43.17L6.32,6.68A.25.25,0,0,0,6.32,6.32Z"></path></svg><strong class="copy-button-label"></strong><span class="tooltip hidden"> Copy to Clipboard</span>
</div></button>
<div class="content">
<pre class="rouge highlight"><code data-lang="console"><span class="gp">$</span><span class="w"> </span>cvlc <span class="nt">--play-and-exit</span> <span class="nt">--drm-vout-display</span> &lt;drm-device&gt; big-buck-bunny-1080p.mp4</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
</li>
<li>
<p>Replace the <code>&lt;drm-device&gt;</code> placeholder with one of the following options:</p>
<table class="tableblock frame-all grid-all stretch"><colgroup><col style="width: 50%;"><col style="width: 50%;"></colgroup><tbody><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">DRM device</p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">Description
+</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>HDMI-A-1</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">The HDMI output on a Raspberry Pi Zero, or Raspberry Pi Model 1, 2 or 3; <strong>or</strong> the HDMI0 output on a Raspberry Pi 4B and later, and all Keyboard models
+</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>HDMI-A-2</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">The HDMI1 output on Raspberry Pi 4B and later, Compute Module CM4 and later (excluding CM4S), and all Keyboard models
+</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>DSI-1</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">The Raspberry Pi Touch Display, Raspberry Pi Touch Display 2, or third-party DSI screen
+</p></td>
</tr><tr><td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock"><code>DSI-2</code></p></td>
<td class="tableblock halign-left valign-top"><p class="tableblock">The second DSI output for models with multiple DSI ports (Flagship models since Raspberry Pi 5, and all Compute Module models)
+</p></td>
</tr></tbody></table></li>
</ol></div>
<div class="admonitionblock tip">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Tip</div>
</td>
<td class="content">
<div class="paragraph">
<p>Use the following command to get a list of all DRM devices on your Raspberry Pi.</p>
</div>
<div class="admonitionblock note">
<table><tr><td class="icon">
<div class="title">Note</div>
</td>
<td class="content">
using <code>kmsprint</code> without parameters only queries the first card found. Therefore this might not list all attached displays on Raspberry Pi 5 and later models. See <a href="../computers/os.html#kmsprint">kmsprint.</a>
</td>
</tr></table></div>
<div class="listingblock">
<button class="copy-button hidden" aria-label="copy code to clipboard" type="button" aria-hidden="true"><div class="copy-button-inner">
